12. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Controls;
13. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Input;
14. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Media;
15. 
16. namespace SDKTemplate
17. {
18.     public sealed partial class Scenario1 : Page
19.     {
20.         public Scenario1()
21.         {
22.             this.InitializeComponent();
23. 
24.             // pointer press/release handlers
25.             pressedTarget.PointerPressed += new PointerEventHandler(target_PointerPressed);
26.             pressedTarget.PointerReleased += new PointerEventHandler(target_PointerReleased);
27. 
28.             // pointer enter/exit handlers
29.             enterExitTarget.PointerEntered += new PointerEventHandler(target_PointerEntered);
30.             enterExitTarget.PointerExited += new PointerEventHandler(target_PointerExited);
31. 
32.             // gesture handlers
33.             tapTarget.Tapped += new TappedEventHandler(target_Tapped);
34.             tapTarget.DoubleTapped += new DoubleTappedEventHandler(target_DoubleTapped);
35. 
36.             holdTarget.Holding += new HoldingEventHandler(target_Holding);
37.             holdTarget.RightTapped += new RightTappedEventHandler(target_RightTapped);
38.         }
39. 
40.         // A PointerPressed event is sent whenever a mouse button, finger, or pen is pressed to make
41.         // contact with an object
42.         void target_PointerPressed(object sender, PointerRoutedEventArgs e)
43.         {
44.             pressedTarget.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.RoyalBlue);
45.             pressedTargetText.Text = "Pointer Pressed";
46.         }
47. 
48.         // A PointerReleased event is sent whenever a mouse button, finger, or pen is released to remove
49.         // contact from an object
50.         void target_PointerReleased(object sender, PointerRoutedEventArgs e)
51.         {
52.             pressedTarget.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.LightGray);
53.             pressedTargetText.Text = "Pointer Released";
54.         }
55. 
56.         // A PointerEntered event is sent whenever a mouse cursor is moved on top of an object
57.         // or when a pen or finger is dragged on top of an object
58.         void target_PointerEntered(object sender, PointerRoutedEventArgs e)
59.         {
60.             enterExitTarget.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.RoyalBlue);
61.             enterExitTargetText.Text = "Pointer Entered";
62.         }
63. 
64.         // A PointerExited event is sent whenever a mouse cursor is moved off of an object
65.         // or when a pen or finger is dragged off of an object
66.         void target_PointerExited(object sender, PointerRoutedEventArgs e)
67.         {
68.             enterExitTarget.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.LightGray);
69.             enterExitTargetText.Text = "Pointer Exited";
70.         }
71. 
72.         // A Tapped event is sent whenever a mouse is clicked or a finger or pen taps
73.         // the object
74.         void target_Tapped(object sender, TappedRoutedEventArgs e)
75.         {
76.             tapTarget.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.DeepSkyBlue);
77.             tapTargetText.Text = "Tapped";
78.         }
79. 
80.         // A DoubleTapped event is sent whenever a mouse is double-clicked or a finger or pen taps
81.         // the object twice in quick succession
82.         void target_DoubleTapped(object sender, DoubleTappedRoutedEventArgs e)
83.         {
84.             tapTarget.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.RoyalBlue);
85.             tapTargetText.Text = "Double-Tapped";
86.         }
87. 
88.         // A RightTapped event is sent whenever a mouse is right-clicked or a finger or pen
89.         // completes a Holding event.  This is intended to be used to handle secondary actions
90.         // on an object.
91.         void target_RightTapped(object sender, RightTappedRoutedEventArgs e)
92.         {
93.             holdTarget.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.RoyalBlue);
94.             holdTargetText.Text = "Right Tapped";
95.         }
96. 
97.         // A Holding event is sent whenever a finger or pen is pressed and held on top of
98.         // an object.
99.         // Once a small amount of time has elapsed, the event is sent with a HoldingState
100.         // of the type HoldingState.Started, indicating that the held threshold has just
101.         // been passed.
102.         // When a finger has been lifted after a successful hold, a Holding event is sent
103.         // with a HoldingState of Completed. 
104.         // If the user cancels the hold after it has been started, but before it completes,
105.         // a Holding event is sent with a HoldingState of Canceled.
106.         void target_Holding(object sender, HoldingRoutedEventArgs e)
107.         {
108.             if (e.HoldingState == Windows.UI.Input.HoldingState.Started)
109.             {
110.                 holdTarget.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.DeepSkyBlue);
111.                 holdTargetText.Text = "Holding";
112.             }
113.             else if (e.HoldingState == Windows.UI.Input.HoldingState.Completed)
114.             {
115.                 holdTarget.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.LightGray);
116.                 holdTargetText.Text = "Held";
117.             }
118.             else
119.             {
120.                 holdTarget.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.LightGray);
121.                 holdTargetText.Text = "Hold Canceled";
122.             }
123.         }
124.     }
125. }

50.         {

52. 
53.             // Determine if the point that entered the canvas is "in contact" with the
54.             // screen (mouse button pressed, finger down, or pen down).
55.             // This will be encountered if the user touches down in the app surface,
56.             // drags their finger off of the app, and drags it back onto the app without
57.             // lifting their finger.
58.             if (currentPoint.IsInContact)
59.             {
60.                 CreateOrUpdatePropertyPopUp(currentPoint);
61.             }
62.         }

108.         // Return the properties that are common to all pointers
109.         String GetPointerProperties(Windows.UI.Input.PointerPoint currentPoint)
110.         {
111.             System.Text.StringBuilder sb = new System.Text.StringBuilder();
112. 
113.             sb.Append("ID: " + currentPoint.PointerId);
114.             sb.Append("\nX: " + currentPoint.Position.X);
115.             sb.Append("\nY: " + currentPoint.Position.Y);
116.             sb.Append("\nContact: " + currentPoint.IsInContact + "\n");
117. 
118.             return sb.ToString(); ;
119.         }

121.         String GetDeviceSpecificProperties(Windows.UI.Input.PointerPoint currentPoint)
122.         {
123.             String deviceSpecificProperties = "";
124. 
125.             // Detect the type of device being used by examining PointerDeviceType
126.             // to distinguish between mouse, touch, and pen
127.             switch (currentPoint.PointerDevice.PointerDeviceType)
128.             {
129.                 case Windows.Devices.Input.PointerDeviceType.Mouse:
130.                     deviceSpecificProperties = GetMouseProperties(currentPoint);
131.                     break;
132.                 case Windows.Devices.Input.PointerDeviceType.Pen:
133.                     deviceSpecificProperties = GetPenProperties(currentPoint);
134.                     break;
135.                 case Windows.Devices.Input.PointerDeviceType.Touch:
136.                     deviceSpecificProperties = GetTouchProperties(currentPoint);
137.                     break;
138.             }
139. 
140.             return deviceSpecificProperties;
141.         }

186.         //
187.         // Popup UI code below
188.         //
189.         void RenderPropertyPopUp(String pointerProperties, String deviceSpecificProperties, Windows.UI.Input.PointerPoint currentPoint)
190.         {
191.             if (popups.ContainsKey(currentPoint.PointerId))
192.             {
193.                 TextBlock pointerText = (TextBlock)popups[currentPoint.PointerId].Children[0];
194.                 pointerText.Text = pointerProperties;
195.                 TextBlock deviceSpecificText = (TextBlock)popups[currentPoint.PointerId].Children[1];
196.                 deviceSpecificText.Text = deviceSpecificProperties;
197.             }
198.             else
199.             {
200.                 StackPanel pointerPanel = new StackPanel();
201.                 TextBlock pointerText = new TextBlock();
202.                 pointerText.Text = pointerProperties;
203.                 pointerPanel.Children.Add(pointerText);
204. 
205.                 TextBlock deviceSpecificText = new TextBlock();
206.                 deviceSpecificText.Text = deviceSpecificProperties;
207. 
208.                 // Detect the type of device being used by examining PointerDeviceType
209.                 // to distinguish between mouse, touch, and pen
210.                 switch (currentPoint.PointerDevice.PointerDeviceType)
211.                 {
212.                     case Windows.Devices.Input.PointerDeviceType.Mouse:
213.                         deviceSpecificText.Foreground = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Red);
214.                         break;
215. 
216.                     case Windows.Devices.Input.PointerDeviceType.Touch:
217.                         deviceSpecificText.Foreground = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Green);
218.                         break;
219. 
220.                     case Windows.Devices.Input.PointerDeviceType.Pen:
221.                         deviceSpecificText.Foreground = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Yellow);
222.                         break;
223.                 }
224.                 pointerPanel.Children.Add(deviceSpecificText);
225.                 popups[currentPoint.PointerId] = pointerPanel;
226.                 mainCanvas.Children.Add(popups[currentPoint.PointerId]);
227.             }
228.             TranslateTransform transform = new TranslateTransform();
229.             transform.X = currentPoint.Position.X + 24;
230.             transform.Y = currentPoint.Position.Y + 24;
231.             popups[currentPoint.PointerId].RenderTransform = transform;
232.         }
233. 
234.         void HidePropertyPopUp(Windows.UI.Input.PointerPoint currentPoint)
235.         {
236.             if (popups.ContainsKey(currentPoint.PointerId))
237.             {
238.                 mainCanvas.Children.Remove(popups[currentPoint.PointerId]);
239.                 popups[currentPoint.PointerId] = null;
240.                 popups.Remove(currentPoint.PointerId);
241.             }
242.         }

12. using Windows.Foundation;
13. using Windows.UI.Xaml;
14. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Controls;
15. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Input;
16. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Media;
17. 
18. namespace SDKTemplate
19. {
20.     public sealed partial class Scenario4 : Page
21.     {
22.         private TransformGroup transforms;
23.         private MatrixTransform previousTransform;
24.         private CompositeTransform deltaTransform;
25.         private bool forceManipulationsToEnd;
26. 
27.         public Scenario4()
28.         {
29.             this.InitializeComponent();
30.             forceManipulationsToEnd = false;
31. 
32.             InitOptions();
33. 
34.             // Initialize the transforms that will be used to manipulate the shape
35.             InitManipulationTransforms();
36. 
37.             // Register for the various manipulation events that will occur on the shape
38.             manipulateMe.ManipulationStarted += new ManipulationStartedEventHandler(ManipulateMe_ManipulationStarted);
39.             manipulateMe.ManipulationDelta += new ManipulationDeltaEventHandler(ManipulateMe_ManipulationDelta);
40.             manipulateMe.ManipulationCompleted += new ManipulationCompletedEventHandler(ManipulateMe_ManipulationCompleted);
41.             manipulateMe.ManipulationInertiaStarting += new ManipulationInertiaStartingEventHandler(ManipulateMe_ManipulationInertiaStarting);
42. 
43.             // The ManipulationMode property dictates what manipulation events the element
44.             // will listen to.  This will set it to a limited subset of these events.
45.             manipulateMe.ManipulationMode =
46.                 ManipulationModes.TranslateX |
47.                 ManipulationModes.TranslateY |
48.                 ManipulationModes.Rotate |
49.                 ManipulationModes.TranslateInertia |
50.                 ManipulationModes.RotateInertia;
51.         }
52. 
53.         private void InitManipulationTransforms()
54.         {
55.             transforms = new TransformGroup();
56.             previousTransform = new MatrixTransform() { Matrix = Matrix.Identity };
57.             deltaTransform = new CompositeTransform();
58. 
59.             transforms.Children.Add(previousTransform);
60.             transforms.Children.Add(deltaTransform);
61. 
62.             // Set the render transform on the rect
63.             manipulateMe.RenderTransform = transforms;
64.         }
65. 
66.         // When a manipulation begins, change the color of the object to reflect
67.         // that a manipulation is in progress
68.         void ManipulateMe_ManipulationStarted(object sender, ManipulationStartedRoutedEventArgs e)
69.         {
70.             forceManipulationsToEnd = false;
71.             manipulateMe.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.DeepSkyBlue);
72.         }
73. 
74.         // Process the change resulting from a manipulation
75.         void ManipulateMe_ManipulationDelta(object sender, ManipulationDeltaRoutedEventArgs e)
76.         {
77.             // If the reset button has been pressed, mark the manipulation as completed
78.             if (forceManipulationsToEnd)
79.             {
80.                 e.Complete();
81.                 return;
82.             }
83. 
84.             previousTransform.Matrix = transforms.Value;
85. 
86.             // Get center point for rotation
87.             Point center = previousTransform.TransformPoint(new Point(e.Position.X, e.Position.Y));
88.             deltaTransform.CenterX = center.X;
89.             deltaTransform.CenterY = center.Y;
90. 
91.             // Look at the Delta property of the ManipulationDeltaRoutedEventArgs to retrieve
92.             // the rotation, scale, X, and Y changes
93.             deltaTransform.Rotation = e.Delta.Rotation;
94.             deltaTransform.TranslateX = e.Delta.Translation.X;
95.             deltaTransform.TranslateY = e.Delta.Translation.Y;
96.         }
97. 
98.         // When a manipulation that's a result of inertia begins, change the color of the
99.         // the object to reflect that inertia has taken over
100.         void ManipulateMe_ManipulationInertiaStarting(object sender, ManipulationInertiaStartingRoutedEventArgs e)
101.         {
102.             manipulateMe.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.RoyalBlue);
103.         }
104. 
105.         // When a manipulation has finished, reset the color of the object
106.         void ManipulateMe_ManipulationCompleted(object sender, ManipulationCompletedRoutedEventArgs e)
107.         {
108.             manipulateMe.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.LightGray);
109.         }
110. 
111.         private void movementAxis_Changed(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
112.         {
113.             // Set the object to listen to both X and Y translation events
114.             manipulateMe.ManipulationMode |= ManipulationModes.TranslateX | ManipulationModes.TranslateY;
115. 
116.             ComboBoxItem selectedItem = (ComboBoxItem)((ComboBox)sender).SelectedItem;
117.             switch (selectedItem.Content.ToString())
118.             {
119.                 case "X only":
120.                     // Set the object to not listen for translations on the Y axis
121.                     manipulateMe.ManipulationMode ^= ManipulationModes.TranslateY;
122.                     break;
123.                 case "Y only":
124.                     // Set the object to not listen for translations on the X axis
125.                     manipulateMe.ManipulationMode ^= ManipulationModes.TranslateX;
126.                     break;
127.             }
128.         }
129. 
130.         private void InertiaSwitch_Toggled(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
131.         {
132.             // Flip the current TranslateInertia and RotateInertia values in response to the
133.             // InertiaSwitch being toggled
134.             if (manipulateMe != null)
135.             {
136.                 manipulateMe.ManipulationMode ^= ManipulationModes.TranslateInertia | ManipulationModes.RotateInertia;
137.             }
138.         }
139. 
140.         //
141.         // More UI code below
142.         //
143.         private void InitOptions()
144.         {
145.             movementAxis.SelectedIndex = 0;
146.             inertiaSwitch.IsOn = true;
147.         }
148. 
149.         void resetButton_Pressed(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
150.         {
151.             forceManipulationsToEnd = true;
152.             manipulateMe.RenderTransform = null;
153.             movementAxis.SelectedIndex = 0;
154.             InitOptions();
155.             InitManipulationTransforms();
156.         }
157.     }
158. }

45.         }
46. 
47.         private void movementAxis_Changed(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
48.         {
49.             if (manipulationProcessor == null)
50.             {
51.                 return;
52.             }
53. 
54.             ComboBoxItem selectedItem = (ComboBoxItem)((ComboBox)sender).SelectedItem;
55.             switch (selectedItem.Content.ToString())
56.             {
57.                 case "X only":
58.                     manipulationProcessor.LockToXAxis();
59.                     break;
60.                 case "Y only":
61.                     manipulationProcessor.LockToYAxis();
62.                     break;
63.                 default:
64.                     manipulationProcessor.MoveOnXAndYAxes();
65.                     break;
66.             }
67.         }
68. 
69.         private void InertiaSwitch_Toggled(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
70.         {
71.             if (manipulationProcessor == null)
72.             {
73.                 return;
74.             }
75. 
76.             manipulationProcessor.UseInertia(InertiaSwitch.IsOn);
77.         }
78. 
79.         void resetButton_Pressed(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
80.         {
81.             InitOptions();
82.             manipulationProcessor.Reset();
83.         }
84.     }
85. 
86.     class ManipulationInputProcessor
87.     {
88.         GestureRecognizer recognizer;
89.         UIElement element;
90.         UIElement reference;
91.         TransformGroup cumulativeTransform;
92.         MatrixTransform previousTransform;
93.         CompositeTransform deltaTransform;
94. 
95.         public ManipulationInputProcessor(GestureRecognizer gestureRecognizer, UIElement target, UIElement referenceFrame)
96.         {
97.             recognizer = gestureRecognizer;
98.             element = target;
99.             reference = referenceFrame;
100. 
101.             // Initialize the transforms that will be used to manipulate the shape
102.             InitializeTransforms();
103. 
104.             // The GestureSettings property dictates what manipulation events the
105.             // Gesture Recognizer will listen to.  This will set it to a limited
106.             // subset of these events.
107.             recognizer.GestureSettings = GenerateDefaultSettings();
108. 
109.             // Set up pointer event handlers. These receive input events that are used by the gesture recognizer.
110.             element.PointerPressed += OnPointerPressed;
111.             element.PointerMoved += OnPointerMoved;
112.             element.PointerReleased += OnPointerReleased;
113.             element.PointerCanceled += OnPointerCanceled;
114. 
115.             // Set up event handlers to respond to gesture recognizer output
116.             recognizer.ManipulationStarted += OnManipulationStarted;
117.             recognizer.ManipulationUpdated += OnManipulationUpdated;
118.             recognizer.ManipulationCompleted += OnManipulationCompleted;
119.             recognizer.ManipulationInertiaStarting += OnManipulationInertiaStarting;
120.         }
121. 
122.         public void InitializeTransforms()
123.         {
124.             cumulativeTransform = new TransformGroup();
125.             deltaTransform = new CompositeTransform();
126.             previousTransform = new MatrixTransform() { Matrix = Matrix.Identity };
127. 
128.             cumulativeTransform.Children.Add(previousTransform);
129.             cumulativeTransform.Children.Add(deltaTransform);
130. 
131.             element.RenderTransform = cumulativeTransform;
132.         }
133. 
134.         // Return the default GestureSettings for this sample
135.         GestureSettings GenerateDefaultSettings()
136.         {
137.             return GestureSettings.ManipulationTranslateX |
138.                 GestureSettings.ManipulationTranslateY |
139.                 GestureSettings.ManipulationRotate |
140.                 GestureSettings.ManipulationTranslateInertia |
141.                 GestureSettings.ManipulationRotateInertia;
142.         }
143. 
144.         // Route the pointer pressed event to the gesture recognizer.
145.         // The points are in the reference frame of the canvas that contains the rectangle element.
146.         void OnPointerPressed(object sender, PointerRoutedEventArgs args)
147.         {
148.             // Set the pointer capture to the element being interacted with so that only it
149.             // will fire pointer-related events
150.             element.CapturePointer(args.Pointer);
151. 
152.             // Feed the current point into the gesture recognizer as a down event
153.             recognizer.ProcessDownEvent(args.GetCurrentPoint(reference));
154.         }
155. 
156.         // Route the pointer moved event to the gesture recognizer.
157.         // The points are in the reference frame of the canvas that contains the rectangle element.
158.         void OnPointerMoved(object sender, PointerRoutedEventArgs args)
159.         {
160.             // Feed the set of points into the gesture recognizer as a move event
161.             recognizer.ProcessMoveEvents(args.GetIntermediatePoints(reference));
162.         }
163. 
164.         // Route the pointer released event to the gesture recognizer.
165.         // The points are in the reference frame of the canvas that contains the rectangle element.
166.         void OnPointerReleased(object sender, PointerRoutedEventArgs args)
167.         {
168.             // Feed the current point into the gesture recognizer as an up event
169.             recognizer.ProcessUpEvent(args.GetCurrentPoint(reference));
170. 
171.             // Release the pointer
172.             element.ReleasePointerCapture(args.Pointer);
173.         }
174. 
175.         // Route the pointer canceled event to the gesture recognizer.
176.         // The points are in the reference frame of the canvas that contains the rectangle element.
177.         void OnPointerCanceled(object sender, PointerRoutedEventArgs args)
178.         {
179.             recognizer.CompleteGesture();
180.             element.ReleasePointerCapture(args.Pointer);
181.         }
182. 
183.         // When a manipulation begins, change the color of the object to reflect
184.         // that a manipulation is in progress
185.         void OnManipulationStarted(object sender, ManipulationStartedEventArgs e)
186.         {
187.             Border b = element as Border;
188.             b.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.DeepSkyBlue);
189.         }
190. 
191.         // Process the change resulting from a manipulation
192.         void OnManipulationUpdated(object sender, ManipulationUpdatedEventArgs e)
193.         {
194.             previousTransform.Matrix = cumulativeTransform.Value;
195. 
196.             // Get the center point of the manipulation for rotation
197.             Point center = new Point(e.Position.X, e.Position.Y);
198.             deltaTransform.CenterX = center.X;
199.             deltaTransform.CenterY = center.Y;
200. 
201.             // Look at the Delta property of the ManipulationDeltaRoutedEventArgs to retrieve
202.             // the rotation, X, and Y changes
203.             deltaTransform.Rotation = e.Delta.Rotation;
204.             deltaTransform.TranslateX = e.Delta.Translation.X;
205.             deltaTransform.TranslateY = e.Delta.Translation.Y;
206.         }
207. 
208.         // When a manipulation that's a result of inertia begins, change the color of the
209.         // the object to reflect that inertia has taken over
210.         void OnManipulationInertiaStarting(object sender, ManipulationInertiaStartingEventArgs e)
211.         {
212.             Border b = element as Border;
213.             b.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.RoyalBlue);
214.         }
215. 
216.         // When a manipulation has finished, reset the color of the object
217.         void OnManipulationCompleted(object sender, ManipulationCompletedEventArgs e)
218.         {
219.             Border b = element as Border;
220.             b.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.LightGray);
221.         }
222. 
223.         // Modify the GestureSettings property to only allow movement on the X axis
224.         public void LockToXAxis()
225.         {
226.             recognizer.CompleteGesture();
227.             recognizer.GestureSettings |= GestureSettings.ManipulationTranslateY | GestureSettings.ManipulationTranslateX;
228.             recognizer.GestureSettings ^= GestureSettings.ManipulationTranslateY;
229.         }
230. 
231.         // Modify the GestureSettings property to only allow movement on the Y axis
232.         public void LockToYAxis()
233.         {
234.             recognizer.CompleteGesture();
235.             recognizer.GestureSettings |= GestureSettings.ManipulationTranslateY | GestureSettings.ManipulationTranslateX;
236.             recognizer.GestureSettings ^= GestureSettings.ManipulationTranslateX;
237.         }
238. 
239.         // Modify the GestureSettings property to allow movement on both the the X and Y axes
240.         public void MoveOnXAndYAxes()
241.         {
242.             recognizer.CompleteGesture();
243.             recognizer.GestureSettings |= GestureSettings.ManipulationTranslateX | GestureSettings.ManipulationTranslateY;
244.         }
245. 
246.         // Modify the GestureSettings property to enable or disable inertia based on the passed-in value
247.         public void UseInertia(bool inertia)
248.         {
249.             if (!inertia)
250.             {
251.                 recognizer.CompleteGesture();
252.                 recognizer.GestureSettings ^= GestureSettings.ManipulationTranslateInertia | GestureSettings.ManipulationRotateInertia;
253.             }
254.             else
255.             {
256.                 recognizer.GestureSettings |= GestureSettings.ManipulationTranslateInertia | GestureSettings.ManipulationRotateInertia;
257.             }
258.         }
259. 
260.         public void Reset()
261.         {
262.             element.RenderTransform = null;
263.             recognizer.CompleteGesture();
264.             InitializeTransforms();
265.             recognizer.GestureSettings = GenerateDefaultSettings();
266.         }
267.     }
268. }
269.

30.     {

32. 
33.         public MainPage()
34.         {
35.             this.InitializeComponent();
36. 
37.             // This is a static public property that allows downstream pages to get a handle to the MainPage instance
38.             // in order to call methods that are in this class.
39.             Current = this;
40.             SampleTitle.Text = FEATURE_NAME;
41.         }
42. 
43.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
44.         {
45.             // Populate the scenario list from the SampleConfiguration.cs file
46.             var itemCollection = new List<Scenario>();
47.             int i = 1;
48.             foreach (Scenario s in scenarios)
49.             {
50.                 itemCollection.Add(new Scenario { Title = $"{i++}) {s.Title}", ClassType = s.ClassType });
51.             }
52.             ScenarioControl.ItemsSource = itemCollection;
53.

55.             {
56.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = -1;
57.             }
58.             else
59.             {
60.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = 0;
61.             }
62.         }
63. 
64.         /// <summary>
65.         /// Called whenever the user changes selection in the scenarios list.  This method will navigate to the respective
66.         /// sample scenario page.
67.         /// </summary>
68.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
69.         /// <param name="e"></param>
70.         private void ScenarioControl_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
71.         {
72.             // Clear the status block when navigating scenarios.
73.             NotifyUser(String.Empty, NotifyType.StatusMessage);
74.

76.             Scenario s = scenarioListBox.SelectedItem as Scenario;
77.             if (s != null)

82.                     Splitter.IsPaneOpen = false;

92.         /// <summary>
93.         /// Display a message to the user.
94.         /// This method may be called from any thread.
95.         /// </summary>
96.         /// <param name="strMessage"></param>
97.         /// <param name="type"></param>
98.         public void NotifyUser(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
99.         {
100.             // If called from the UI thread, then update immediately.
101.             // Otherwise, schedule a task on the UI thread to perform the update.
102.             if (Dispatcher.HasThreadAccess)
103.             {
104.                 UpdateStatus(strMessage, type);
105.             }
106.             else
107.             {
108.                 var task = Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => UpdateStatus(strMessage, type));
109.             }
110.         }
111. 
112.         private void UpdateStatus(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
113.         {
114.             switch (type)
115.             {
116.                 case NotifyType.StatusMessage:
117.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Green);
118.                     break;
119.                 case NotifyType.ErrorMessage:
120.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Red);
121.                     break;
122.             }
123. 
124.             StatusBlock.Text = strMessage;
125. 
126.             // Collapse the StatusBlock if it has no text to conserve real estate.
127.             StatusBorder.Visibility = (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty) ? Visibility.Visible : Visibility.Collapsed;
128.             if (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty)
129.             {
130.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
131.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
132.             }
133.             else
134.             {
135.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
136.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
137.             }
138. 
139. 			// Raise an event if necessary to enable a screen reader to announce the status update.
140. 			var peer = FrameworkElementAutomationPeer.FromElement(StatusBlock);
141. 			if (peer != null)
142. 			{
143. 				peer.RaiseAutomationEvent(AutomationEvents.LiveRegionChanged);
144. 			}
145. 		}

26.     {
27.         /// <summary>
28.         /// Initializes the singleton application object.  This is the first line of authored code
29.         /// executed, and as such is the logical equivalent of main() or WinMain().
30.         /// </summary>
31.         public App()
32.         {
33.             this.InitializeComponent();
34.             this.Construct();
35.         }
36. 
37.         /// <summary>
38.         /// Invoked when the application is launched normally by the end user.  Other entry points
39.         /// will be used such as when the application is launched to open a specific file.
40.         /// </summary>
41.         /// <param name="e">Details about the launch request and process.</param>
42.         protected override void OnLaunched(LaunchActivatedEventArgs e)
43.         {
44. 
45. #if DEBUG
46.             if (System.Diagnostics.Debugger.IsAttached)
47.             {
48.                 this.DebugSettings.EnableFrameRateCounter = false;
49.             }
50. #endif
51. 
52.             Frame rootFrame = Window.Current.Content as Frame;
53. 
54.             // Do not repeat app initialization when the Window already has content,
55.             // just ensure that the window is active
56.             if (rootFrame == null)
57.             {
58.                 // Create a Frame to act as the navigation context and navigate to the first page
59.                 rootFrame = new Frame();
60.                 // Set the default language
61.                 rootFrame.Language = Windows.Globalization.ApplicationLanguages.Languages[0];
62. 
63.                 rootFrame.NavigationFailed += OnNavigationFailed;
64. 
65.                 if (e.PreviousExecutionState == ApplicationExecutionState.Terminated)
66.                 {
67.                     //TODO: Load state from previously suspended application
68.                 }
69. 
70.                 // Place the frame in the current Window
71.                 Window.Current.Content = rootFrame;
72.             }
73. 
74.             if (rootFrame.Content == null)
75.             {
76.                 // When the navigation stack isn't restored navigate to the first page,
77.                 // configuring the new page by passing required information as a navigation
78.                 // parameter
79.                 rootFrame.Navigate(typeof(MainPage), e.Arguments);
80.             }
81. 
82.             // Ensure the current window is active
83.             Window.Current.Activate();
84. 
85.             // Some samples want access to the LaunchActivatedEventArgs.
86.             LaunchCompleted(e);
87.         }
88. 
89.         private Frame CreateRootFrame()
90.         {
91.             Frame rootFrame = Window.Current.Content as Frame;
92. 
93.             // Do not repeat app initialization when the Window already has content,
94.             // just ensure that the window is active
95.             if (rootFrame == null)
96.             {
97.                 // Create a Frame to act as the navigation context and navigate to the first page
98.                 rootFrame = new Frame();
99. 
100.                 // Set the default language
101.                 rootFrame.Language = Windows.Globalization.ApplicationLanguages.Languages[0];
102.                 rootFrame.NavigationFailed += OnNavigationFailed;
103. 
104.                 // Place the frame in the current Window
105.                 Window.Current.Content = rootFrame;
106.             }
107. 
108.             return rootFrame;
109.         }

1. //*********************************************************

12. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Controls;
13. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Input;
14. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Media;
15. 
16. namespace SDKTemplate
17. {
18.     public sealed partial class Scenario1 : Page
19.     {
20.         public Scenario1()
21.         {
22.             this.InitializeComponent();
23. 
24.             // pointer press/release handlers
25.             pressedTarget.PointerPressed += new PointerEventHandler(target_PointerPressed);
26.             pressedTarget.PointerReleased += new PointerEventHandler(target_PointerReleased);
27. 
28.             // pointer enter/exit handlers
29.             enterExitTarget.PointerEntered += new PointerEventHandler(target_PointerEntered);
30.             enterExitTarget.PointerExited += new PointerEventHandler(target_PointerExited);
31. 
32.             // gesture handlers
33.             tapTarget.Tapped += new TappedEventHandler(target_Tapped);
34.             tapTarget.DoubleTapped += new DoubleTappedEventHandler(target_DoubleTapped);
35. 
36.             holdTarget.Holding += new HoldingEventHandler(target_Holding);
37.             holdTarget.RightTapped += new RightTappedEventHandler(target_RightTapped);
38.         }
39. 
40.         // A PointerPressed event is sent whenever a mouse button, finger, or pen is pressed to make
41.         // contact with an object
42.         void target_PointerPressed(object sender, PointerRoutedEventArgs e)
43.         {
44.             pressedTarget.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.RoyalBlue);
45.             pressedTargetText.Text = "Pointer Pressed";
46.         }
47. 
48.         // A PointerReleased event is sent whenever a mouse button, finger, or pen is released to remove
49.         // contact from an object
50.         void target_PointerReleased(object sender, PointerRoutedEventArgs e)
51.         {
52.             pressedTarget.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.LightGray);
53.             pressedTargetText.Text = "Pointer Released";
54.         }
55. 
56.         // A PointerEntered event is sent whenever a mouse cursor is moved on top of an object
57.         // or when a pen or finger is dragged on top of an object
58.         void target_PointerEntered(object sender, PointerRoutedEventArgs e)
59.         {
60.             enterExitTarget.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.RoyalBlue);
61.             enterExitTargetText.Text = "Pointer Entered";
62.         }
63. 
64.         // A PointerExited event is sent whenever a mouse cursor is moved off of an object
65.         // or when a pen or finger is dragged off of an object
66.         void target_PointerExited(object sender, PointerRoutedEventArgs e)
67.         {
68.             enterExitTarget.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.LightGray);
69.             enterExitTargetText.Text = "Pointer Exited";
70.         }
71. 
72.         // A Tapped event is sent whenever a mouse is clicked or a finger or pen taps
73.         // the object
74.         void target_Tapped(object sender, TappedRoutedEventArgs e)
75.         {
76.             tapTarget.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.DeepSkyBlue);
77.             tapTargetText.Text = "Tapped";
78.         }
79. 
80.         // A DoubleTapped event is sent whenever a mouse is double-clicked or a finger or pen taps
81.         // the object twice in quick succession
82.         void target_DoubleTapped(object sender, DoubleTappedRoutedEventArgs e)
83.         {
84.             tapTarget.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.RoyalBlue);
85.             tapTargetText.Text = "Double-Tapped";
86.         }
87. 
88.         // A RightTapped event is sent whenever a mouse is right-clicked or a finger or pen
89.         // completes a Holding event.  This is intended to be used to handle secondary actions
90.         // on an object.
91.         void target_RightTapped(object sender, RightTappedRoutedEventArgs e)
92.         {
93.             holdTarget.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.RoyalBlue);
94.             holdTargetText.Text = "Right Tapped";
95.         }
96. 
97.         // A Holding event is sent whenever a finger or pen is pressed and held on top of
98.         // an object.
99.         // Once a small amount of time has elapsed, the event is sent with a HoldingState
100.         // of the type HoldingState.Started, indicating that the held threshold has just
101.         // been passed.
102.         // When a finger has been lifted after a successful hold, a Holding event is sent
103.         // with a HoldingState of Completed. 
104.         // If the user cancels the hold after it has been started, but before it completes,
105.         // a Holding event is sent with a HoldingState of Canceled.
106.         void target_Holding(object sender, HoldingRoutedEventArgs e)
107.         {
108.             if (e.HoldingState == Windows.UI.Input.HoldingState.Started)
109.             {
110.                 holdTarget.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.DeepSkyBlue);
111.                 holdTargetText.Text = "Holding";
112.             }
113.             else if (e.HoldingState == Windows.UI.Input.HoldingState.Completed)
114.             {
115.                 holdTarget.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.LightGray);
116.                 holdTargetText.Text = "Held";
117.             }
118.             else
119.             {
120.                 holdTarget.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.LightGray);
121.                 holdTargetText.Text = "Hold Canceled";
122.             }
123.         }
124.     }
125. }

<a id="windowing"></a>
## Windowing: Window.Current / ApplicationView / CoreWindow → AppWindow`Window.Current`, `ApplicationView`, and `CoreWindow` are gone. Track the main window yourself and use `Microsoft.UI.Windowing.AppWindow`. See the official [windowing guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/windowing).UWP (replace this):```csharp
var win = Window.Current;
ApplicationView.GetForCurrentView().TryResizeView(new Size(800, 600));
```WinUI 3 — expose `MainWindow` from `App`:```csharp
public partial class App : Application
{
    // With <Nullable>enable</Nullable>, declare as nullable since the assignment only happens during OnLaunched.
    public static Window? MainWindow { get; private set; }    protected override void OnLaunched(LaunchActivatedEventArgs args)
    {
        var window = new MainWindow();
        MainWindow = window;
        window.Activate();
    }
}
```Consumers running after `OnLaunched` can safely null-forgive:```csharp
var hwnd = WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(App.MainWindow!);
```Resize via `AppWindow`:```csharp
var hwnd = WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(App.MainWindow);
var windowId = Microsoft.UI.Win32Interop.GetWindowIdFromWindow(hwnd);
var appWindow = AppWindow.GetFromWindowId(windowId);
appWindow.Resize(new SizeInt32(800, 600));
```### Initialization order — keep MainWindow's constructor inert`App.MainWindow` (or any equivalent static window reference) is `null` for the entire duration of `new MainWindow()` — the right-hand side runs to completion BEFORE the assignment happens. Anything inside that constructor that reads the static — directly or transitively — sees `null` and throws `E_POINTER` (0x80004003) at startup. The build is clean; the analyzer is silent; only a runtime launch reveals it.APIs that commonly trip this race when called from a `Page` reached during `MainWindow` construction:- `WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(App.MainWindow)`
- `XamlRoot` / `Content.XamlRoot` access through a static-window helper
- `PrintManagerInterop.GetForWindow(hwnd)` / `ShowPrintUIForWindowAsync(hwnd)`
- `FileOpenPicker` / `FileSavePicker` / `FolderPicker` (require an HWND)
- `ContentDialog.ShowAsync()` when the dialog's `XamlRoot` is sourced from a static
- `AppWindow.GetFromWindowId(...)` chained through `App.MainWindow`
- Any custom title bar / `SetTitleBar` helper that resolves the HWND staticallySafe pattern — assign before activate, defer first navigation:```csharp
// App.xaml.cs
protected override void OnLaunched(LaunchActivatedEventArgs args)
{
    var window = new MainWindow();
    MainWindow = window;
    window.Activate();
}
```

The `MainWindow` constructor must do only `InitializeComponent` and inert setup — no navigation to a `Page` that reads `
`App.MainWindow`. Defer the first navigation until after `Activate` completes, either from a `Loaded` handler hooked in `
`InitializeComponent` or by exposing a `NavigateToInitialPage()` method and calling it from `App.OnLaunched` after the st
tatic is assigned.

```csharp
// MainWindow.xaml.cs
public MainWindow()
{
    InitializeComponent();
    this.Activated += (_, _) =>
    {
        if (!_navigated)
        {
            _navigated = true;
            RootFrame.Navigate(typeof(MainPage));
        }
    };
}
private bool _navigated;
```

Validator catches this race with a 10s smoke launch after the build healthcheck passes — see `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1`
` Section 7.

### AppWindow API replacements

| UWP API | WinUI 3 API |
|---------|-------------|
| `ApplicationView.TryResizeView` | `AppWindow.Resize` |
| `AppWindow.TryCreateAsync` | `AppWindow.Create` |
| `AppWindow.TryShowAsync` | `AppWindow.Show` |
| `AppWindow.TryConsolidateAsync` | `AppWindow.Destroy` |
| `AppWindow.RequestMoveXxx` | `AppWindow.Move` |
| `AppWindow.RequestPresentation` | `AppWindow.SetPresenter` |
| `CoreApplicationViewTitleBar` | `AppWindowTitleBar` |
| `CoreApplicationView.TitleBar.ExtendViewIntoTitleBar` | `AppWindow.TitleBar.ExtendsContentIntoTitleBar` |
---
<a id="threading"></a>
## Threading: CoreDispatcher → DispatcherQueue

UWP (replace this):

```csharp
await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => StatusText.Text = "Done");
```

WinUI 3:

```csharp
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() => StatusText.Text = "Done");
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(DispatcherQueuePriority.High, () => ProgressBar.Value = 100);
```

Cache the queue off the UI thread via `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()`. UWP's ASTA reentrancy protection is gone 
 — watch for reentrancy in async code that pumps messages. See the official [threading guide](https://learn.microsoft.com
m/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/threading).

<a id="csproj"></a>
## Project File Updates- Target a current WinAppSDK-supported TFM. The exact TFM is the source of truth in the project's `.csproj`; do not hard-code it across instruction files. Typical values at time of writing:
  - `net8.0-windows10.0.19041.0` (LTS)
  - `net9.0-windows10.0.19041.0`
  - `net10.0-windows10.0.26100.0` (current `dotnet new winui` default in this repo)
- Add `<UseWinUI>true</UseWinUI>`.
- Add `<EnableMsixTooling>true</EnableMsixTooling>` for packaged builds.
- Reference `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK` and `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools`.
- Reference `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools.WinApp` to wire `dotnet run` into `winapp run`.
- Keep `Package.appxmanifest` for packaged scenarios; set `<WindowsPackageType>None</WindowsPackageType>` for unpackaged.### PackageReference reconciliation cheat-sheet`Initialize-UwpMigration.ps1` preserves the UWP `.csproj` at `<Target>/.uwp-source/` and leaves the WinUI 3 scaffold's `.csproj` intact. Open both side-by-side and merge:- **Drop** (UWP-only — never carry over):
  - `Microsoft.NETCore.UniversalWindowsPlatform`
  - Any `<PackageReference>` whose version string contains `uap` or whose name starts with `Windows.SDK.Contracts`.
- **Keep** (already in the scaffold — do not duplicate or downgrade):
  - `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK`
  - `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools`
- **Add** (project-specific — copy across, then update to a WinAppSDK-compatible version):
  - Third-party libraries the UWP project pulled in (`CommunityToolkit.*`, `Microsoft.Extensions.*`, `Newtonsoft.Json`, etc.). Pick the latest stable release; UWP-pinned versions are usually too old.Do **not** copy the UWP `.csproj` over the scaffold's. The two formats are incompatible — the UWP csproj carries `<TargetPlatformIdentifier>UAP</TargetPlatformIdentifier>`, `<OutputType>AppContainerExe</OutputType>`, explicit `<Compile Include="...">` items, and `Microsoft.Common.props` imports, none of which build under WinAppSDK.### Package.appxmanifest — reconcile image references with the assets you actually haveThe WinUI 3 scaffold ships with a default `Package.appxmanifest` that references assets the template provides under `Assets/` (`SplashScreen.scale-200.png`, `Square150x150Logo.scale-200.png`, `Square44x44Logo.scale-200.png`, `StoreLogo.png`, `Wide310x150Logo.scale-200.png`, `LockScreenLogo.scale-200.png`). The UWP SDK sample's manifest usually points at sample-branded assets with a `-sdk` suffix (`Splash-sdk.png`, `squareTile-sdk.png`, `SmallTile-sdk.png`, `StoreLogo-sdk.png`).If you copy the UWP manifest's `Logo` / `Square150x150Logo` / `Square44x44Logo` / `<uap:SplashScreen Image="...">` / `<uap:DefaultTile Wide310x150Logo="...">` references over the scaffold's without also bringing the matching files, **`winapp run` will register the package but the AppX layout will fail to launch** with errors like:```
AppxManifest.xml(...): error 0x80070002: ... splash screen image [Splash-sdk.png] cannot be located.
```(Failure code on the AppX side is typically `0x80073CF6` — "package could not be registered".)You have two options. Pick one **before** the first `winapp run`:- **Preferred — keep the scaffold's manifest image references** (`Assets\SplashScreen.scale-200.png`, etc.). When you merge any other content from the UWP manifest into the scaffold's (capabilities, file-type associations, `<uap:Extension>`, `<Application Id>`, etc.), do **not** overwrite the `Logo`, `<uap:SplashScreen Image>`, `Square150x150Logo`, `Square44x44Logo`, or `Wide310x150Logo` attribute values. The scaffold's defaults already match the files in its `Assets/` folder.- **Alternative — keep the UWP sample's branded assets.** Copy every file the UWP manifest references from `.uwp-source/Assets/` (or `.uwp-source/<SampleName>/Assets/`) into the new project's `Assets/` folder, preserving the exact filename (including the `-sdk` suffix and any scale qualifiers). Verify by re-reading each `Image=`/`Logo>` value in the manifest and confirming `Test-Path "Assets\<that filename>"` for every one of them. Don't rename files to look prettier — the manifest reference is the source of truth.Either way, before the first `winapp run`, sanity-check every asset reference in the manifest resolves:

```powershell
[xml]$m = Get-Content .\Package.appxmanifest
@($m.Package.Properties.Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.Square150x150Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.Square44x44Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.uap_SplashScreen.Image,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.uap_DefaultTile.Wide310x150Logo
) | Where-Object { $_ } | ForEach-Object {
    if (-not (Test-Path $_)) { Write-Host "[MISSING] $_" -ForegroundColor Red }
}
```

### Manifest migration checklist (Windows.Desktop + runFullTrust)

The default UWP `Package.appxmanifest` declares itself as a Universal app, but a packaged WinUI 3 desktop app is a Win32
2 process with package identity — it needs a different shape. `winapp run` will register the AppX but **fail to deploy** 
 with `0x80073CF6` or "requires runFullTrust capability" if any of the following are missing.

When merging the UWP manifest into the scaffold's, make sure all of these are true:

1. **TargetDeviceFamily is `Windows.Desktop`** — not `Windows.Universal`:
   ```xml
   <Dependencies>
     <TargetDeviceFamily Name="Windows.Desktop" MinVersion="10.0.17763.0" MaxVersionTested="10.0.22621.0" />
   </Dependencies>
   ```
   Windows.Universal is UWP-only and rejected for a Win32 entrypoint.

2. **rescap namespace declared on `<Package>` and added to `IgnorableNamespaces`**:
   ```xml
   <Package
     xmlns="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/foundation/windows10"
     xmlns:uap="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/uap/windows10"
     xmlns:rescap="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/foundation/windows10/restrictedcapabilities"
     IgnorableNamespaces="uap rescap">
   ```
   Without the namespace declaration, item 3 below is silently stripped by AppX validation and you get the "requires run
nFullTrust" error at deploy time.

3. **`runFullTrust` capability declared**:
   ```xml
   <Capabilities>
     <rescap:Capability Name="runFullTrust" />
   </Capabilities>
   ```
   Packaged WinUI 3 desktop apps run outside the UWP AppContainer sandbox and must declare this. Keep any UWP `<Capabili
ity>` entries you actually use (e.g. `<DeviceCapability Name="webcam" />`) but add the `runFullTrust` line above no matte
er what.

4. **`<Application EntryPoint="$targetentrypoint$">`** — the WinUI 3 scaffold uses an MSBuild placeholder that the build
d resolves to the real entry point. Don't replace it with a literal `<UwpAppName>.App` (that's a UWP entry-point pattern)
).

### WUI analyzer warnings (UWP API residue)

The benchmark's `winapp build` injects the `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK.Analyzers` package, which flags UWP-only APIs that c
compile cleanly under WinUI 3 but throw `COMException` at runtime — typically inside `Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Application.Start
t(...)` before any window can render. The runner sees this as `builds=true, runs=false`, and `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` 
 will FAIL the build healthcheck for each unique warning.

| Rule | Symptom | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `WUI0002` | `Window.Current does not exist in WinUI 3 desktop apps` | Store the `Window` reference in `App.xaml.cs` (`
`App.Window`) and pass it where needed; see "Windowing" above. |
| `WUI0003` | `CoreDispatcher is UWP-only` | Use `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()` and `TryEnqueue(...)`; see "Thr
reading" above. |
| `WUI0004` | `SystemNavigationManager.GetForCurrentView() is UWP-only` | Drop the system back button hookup, or use HWN
ND-based COM interop; see "GetForCurrentView Replacements" above. |

Treat every `warning WUI000\d` line in the build output as a defect — the analyzer does not produce false positives. Sea
arch for the API name in this document for the recommended replacement.

### Bootstrap-neutralized helper classes (filter-prone)

Some SDK Samples ship a shared `Common\NavigationHelper.cs` containing a `RootFrameNavigationHelper` class that wires AL
LT+Left / BrowserBack (virtual key 166) / mouse XButton1+XButton2 to frame back/forward navigation. Its WinUI 3 equivalen
nt requires `Microsoft.UI.Input.InputKeyboardSource.GetKeyState` or P/Invoke `GetAsyncKeyState` plus virtual-key code con
nstants — exactly the shape the model provider's content-safety filter classifies as "keylogger / surveillance" code. Mig
grating it routinely produces `CAPIError: 400 Output blocked by content filtering policy`.

`Initialize-UwpMigration.ps1` handles this automatically: before the agent ever sees the file, the bootstrap rewrites ju
ust the `RootFrameNavigationHelper` class body to a no-op stub (preserving the public ctor with `params object[] args` so
o callers compile no matter what signature they invoke). The audit log records `NEUTRALIZED  class RootFrameNavigationHel
lper`. The mapping row's Notes column is prefixed with `NEUTRALIZED:`.

**You don't need to migrate the neutralized class.** Just leave both it and the call site (typically `new RootFrameNavig
gationHelper(rootFrame)` in `App.xaml.cs`'s `OnLaunched`) untouched — back-nav is not a demonstrated feature of any UWP S
SDK sample. The remainder of `NavigationHelper.cs` (the `NavigationHelper` class used for per-page state save/restore, th
he LoadStateEventArgs / SaveStateEventArgs types) is migrated normally; it does not trigger the filter.

If you really want ALT+Left back-nav, add a single declarative XAML element to the AppBarButton that triggers GoBack:   

```xml
<AppBarButton Icon="Back" Click="OnBackClick">
  <AppBarButton.KeyboardAccelerators>
    <KeyboardAccelerator Key="Left" Modifiers="Menu"/>
  </AppBarButton.KeyboardAccelerators>
</AppBarButton>
```

`KeyboardAccelerator` is declarative and does not trip the filter.

The migration is complete and verified. Here's a summary of what was done:

## Summary

Successfully migrated the **Basic Input** UWP C# sample to WinUI 3. 

**Files created:**
- `Styles/Styles.xaml` — WinUI 3-compatible styles (SampleHeaderTextStyle, ScenarioDescriptionTextStyle, etc.)
- `1-InputEvents.xaml` through `5-GestureRecognizer.xaml` — all 5 scenario XAML pages

**Files migrated:**
- `MainPage.xaml` / `MainPage.xaml.cs` — full SDK sample shell with `SplitView` + `ListBox` + `Frame` navigation in `SDKTemplate` namespace
- `App.xaml` — added styles dictionary merge
- `MainWindow.xaml.cs` — navigates to `SDKTemplate.MainPage` using deferred `Activated` handler
- `Package.appxmanifest` — updated to Windows.Desktop target + `runFullTrust` capability + scaffold's asset references
- `1-InputEvents.xaml.cs` — `Windows.UI.Input.HoldingState` → `Microsoft.UI.Input.HoldingState`
- `2-PointerPointProperties.xaml.cs` — `Windows.UI.Input.PointerPoint/Properties` → `Microsoft.UI.Input.*`, `.PointerDevice.PointerDeviceType` → `.PointerDeviceType`
- `5-GestureRecognizer.xaml.cs` — `using Windows.UI.Input` → `using Microsoft.UI.Input`
- All files: `Windows.UI.Colors` → `Microsoft.UI.Colors`

**Validation:** ✅ `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` PASS — build clean, zero WUI analyzer warnings, all 5 scenarios reachable. App launches and runs successfully.
